7
A Toolkit for Monitoring Networks of Blackboxes in Practice

To demonstrate the practical potential of our approach, we have developed a toolkit based on our framework—see
https://github.com/basvdheuvel/RelaMon

[
10
]
.

The toolkit enhances message-passing web-applications with monitors.
This way, it is possible to add a layer of security when communicating with, e.g., untrusted third-party APIs by monitoring their behavior according to an assumed governing protocol.
The toolkit includes:

1.

A tool, written in Rascal
[
22
,
23
]
, that transpiles protocols specified as well-formed global types to JSON.

2.

A monitor microservice, written in JavaScript, initialized with a protocol specification, a participant ID, the IP-addresses of the unmonitored component and the other components.
The microservice uses relative projection on the supplied JSON protocol specification to construct a finite state machine using
algorithm

2
, which acts as the monitor for the specified participant.

When all components and their respective monitors have been deployed, the monitors perform a handshake such that all components are ready to start executing the protocol.
The monitors forward all correct messages between their respective components and the other monitors in the network, and if needed they send dependency messages.
When a monitor detects an incorrect message, it signals an error to its component and the other monitors.
This way, eventually the entire network becomes aware of the protocol violation, and the execution stops.
It is then up to the components to gracefully deal with the protocol violation, e.g., by reverting to a prior state or restarting the protocol from the start.

The toolkit comes with two test suites:

•

The authorization protocol in
G_{\mathsf{a}}
(
1
), our running example.

•

A weather protocol
G_{\mathsf{w}}
between a client (
c
), a city database (
d
), and a weather API (
w
); we omit curly braces for exchanges with a single branch:

c{!}w\,\mathsf{key}\langle\mathsf{str}\rangle.\mu X.c{!}d\,\mathsf{city}\langle\mathsf{str}\rangle.d{!}c\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\mathsf{coord}\langle\mathsf{str}\rangle.c{!}w\,\mathsf{coord}\langle\mathsf{str}\rangle.w{!}c\,\mathsf{temp}\langle\mathsf{real}\rangle.X,\\
\mathsf{unknown}\langle\rangle.X\end{array}\right\}

This is an interesting test suite, because the weather API (which requires an API
\mathsf{key}
) is not set up to deal with dependencies.
The suite compensates by including a program that acts as a “translator” for the weather API.
The system is then still protected from protocol violations by the weather API.

Definition 16
(Relative Projection with Locations)

Relative projection with Locations
, denoted
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}
, is defined by
algorithm

3
.
This algorithm relies on three auxiliary definitions:

•

The
erasure
of a relative type, denoted
\erase(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)
, is defined by replacing each set of locations in
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
by
\emptyset
(e.g.,
\erase(p{!}q^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}):=p{!}q^{\emptyset}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\erase(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i})\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}
).

•

Given
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
and
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R^{\prime}
such that
\erase(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)=\erase(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R^{\prime})
, we define the
union
of
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
and
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R^{\prime}
, denoted
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R\cup\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R^{\prime}
, by combining each set of locations for each corresponding message in
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
and
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R^{\prime}
(e.g.,
p{!}q^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}\cup p{!}q^{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R^{\prime}_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}:=p{!}q^{\mathbb{L}\cup\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\cup\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R^{\prime}_{i})\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}
).
Given
(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{a})_{a\in A}
for finite
A
such that, for each

\erase(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{a})=\erase(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{b})
, we inductively define
{\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\bigcup}_{a\in A}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{a}
as expected.

•

We define the
appendance
of a label to a set of locations, denoted
\mathbb{L}+\ell

\displaystyle(\mathbb{L}\cup\{\vec{\ell}\})+\ell^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=(\mathbb{L}+\ell^{\prime})\cup\{\vec{\ell},\ell^{\prime}\}

\displaystyle\emptyset+\ell^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\emptyset

We extend this definition to the appendance of a location to a set of locations as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}+(\ell^{\prime},\vec{\ell})

\displaystyle:=(\mathbb{L}+\ell^{\prime})+\vec{\ell}

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}+\varepsilon

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{L}

We extend this definition to the appendance of two sets of locations as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}+\mathbb{L}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:={\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\bigcup}_{\vec{\ell}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\mathbb{L}+\vec{\ell}^{\prime}

Definition 17
(Dependence with Locations)

Given a well-formed global type
G
, we say
p
’s role in
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G

depends on

q
’s role in the initial exchange in
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
, denoted
p\mathbin{\@depsOn}q\mathbin{\@in}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
, if an only if

\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G=s{!}r\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}\{i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{i}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}\}_{i\in I}\wedge p\notin\{s,r\}\wedge q\in\{s,r\}\wedge\exists i,j\in I.~\erase(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{i}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q))\neq\erase(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{j}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)).

8.2
Unfolding Recursive Types and Monitors

When unfolding recursive relative types, the location annotations require care.
Consider, for example

\mu X^{\{1,2\}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.p{!}q^{\{1,2\}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace\ell_{1}\langle T_{1}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.q{!}p^{\{1,2,\ell_{1}\}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace\ell_{2}\langle T_{2}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.X^{\{1,2,\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42},\ell^{\prime}\langle T^{\prime}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.X^{\{1,2,\ell^{\prime}\}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace.

To unfold this type, we should replace each recursive call on
X
with a copy of the whole recursive definition.
However, this is insufficient: the locations of the original recursive definition (starting at
1,2
) do not concur with the locations of the recursive calls (
1,2,\ell_{1},\ell_{2}
and
1,2,\ell^{\prime}
).
Thus, we need to update the locations of the copied recursive calls by inserting the new path behind the location of the original recursive definition.
This way, the recursive call at
1,2,\ell_{1},\ell_{2}
would get replaced by (inserted locations are underlined)

\mu X^{\{1,2,\underline{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.p{!}q^{\{1,2,\underline{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace\ell_{1}\langle T_{1}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.q{!}p^{\{1,2,\underline{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}},\ell_{1}\}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace\ell_{2}\langle T_{2}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.X^{\{1,2,\underline{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}},\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42},\ell^{\prime}\langle T^{\prime}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.X^{\{1,2,\underline{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}},\ell^{\prime}\}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace.

We formally define the unfolding of a relative type
\mu X^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
by removing the prefix
\mathbb{L}
from the locations in
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
(using
\remPref
), replacing each recursive call
X^{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
(where
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}
is the location of the replaced recursive call) with the recursive definition beginning at location
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}
(using
\prepend
), and then replacing the original location
\mathbb{L}
(using
\prepend
).

Definition 18
(Manipulation and Comparison of Locations)

•

Given locations
\vec{\ell}
and
\vec{\ell}^{\prime}
, we say
\vec{\ell}^{\prime}
is a
prefix
of
\vec{\ell}
, denoted
\vec{\ell}^{\prime}\leq\vec{\ell}
, if there exists a
suffix

\vec{\ell}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\vec{\ell}=\vec{\ell}^{\prime},\vec{\ell}^{\prime\prime}
.

•

We say
\vec{\ell}^{\prime}
is a
strict prefix
of
\vec{\ell}
, denoted
\vec{\ell}^{\prime}<\vec{\ell}
, if
\vec{\ell}^{\prime}\leq\vec{\ell}
with suffix
\vec{\ell}^{\prime\prime}\neq\varepsilon
.
We extend the prefix relation to sets of locations as follows:
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathbb{L}
iff
\forall\vec{\ell}\in\mathbb{L}.~\exists\vec{\ell}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}.~\vec{\ell}^{\prime}\leq\vec{\ell}
, i.e., each location in
\mathbb{L}
is prefixed by a location in
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}
.

•

Given
\vec{\ell}\neq\varepsilon
, we write
\fst(\vec{\ell})
to denote the first element of
\vec{\ell}
; formally, there exists
\vec{\ell}^{\prime}
such that
\fst(\vec{\ell}),\vec{\ell}^{\prime}=\vec{\ell}
.

•

Given a set of locations
\mathbb{L}
and a relative type
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
, we define the
prependance
of
\mathbb{L}
to the locations in
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
, denoted
\prepend(\mathbb{L},\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)
, by prepending
\mathbb{L}
to each location in
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
inductively; e.g.,

\prepend(\mathbb{L},p{!}q^{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}):=p{!}q^{\mathbb{L}+\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\prepend(\mathbb{L},\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i})\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}.

•

Given a relative type
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
, we define its
first location
, denoted
\fstLoc(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)
, as the location annotation on the first exchange in
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
; e.g.,
\fstLoc(p{!}q^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}):=\mathbb{L}
.

•

Given a set of locations
\mathbb{L}
and a relative type
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
, we define the
removal of prefix

\mathbb{L}
from the locations in
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
, denoted
\remPref(\mathbb{L},\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)
.
Formally, it checks each location in
\mathbb{L}
as a possible prefix of each location in the set of locations of each exchange of
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
, and leaves only the suffix; e.g.,
\remPref(\mathbb{L},p{!}q^{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}):=p{!}q^{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\remPref(\mathbb{L},\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i})\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}
, where
\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}:=\{\vec{\ell}^{\prime\prime}\mid\exists\vec{\ell}\in\mathbb{L},\vec{\ell}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}.~\vec{\ell}\leq\vec{\ell}^{\prime}\text{ with suffix }\vec{\ell}^{\prime\prime}\}
.

Definition 19
(Unfold Relative Type)

Given a relative type
\mu X^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
, we define its
one-level unfolding
, denoted
\unfold_{1}(\mu X^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)
, as follows:

\unfold_{1}(\mu X^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R):=\prepend(\mathbb{L},\remPref(\mathbb{L},\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)\{\prepend(\mathbb{L}^{\prime},\remPref(\mathbb{L},\mu X^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R))/X^{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\})

Given a relative type
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R
, we define its
(full) unfolding
, denoted
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)
, as follows:

\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R):=\begin{cases}\unfold_{1}(\mu X^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R^{\prime}))&\text{if $\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R=\mu X^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R^{\prime}$}\\
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Lemma 1

For any well-formed global type
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
, participants
p,q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
, and set of locations
\mathbb{L}
,

\displaystyle\prepend(\mathbb{L},\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\varepsilon\}})

\displaystyle=\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}},

\displaystyle\remPref(\mathbb{L},\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}})

\displaystyle=\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\varepsilon\}}.

Proof

By definition.

∎

Definition 20
(Unfold Global Type)

Given a well-formed global type
\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
, we define its
one-level unfolding
:
\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G):=\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\{\mu X\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G/X\}
.
Given a well-formed global type
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
, we define its
(full) unfolding
, denoted
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
, as follows:

\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G):=\begin{cases}\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}))&\text{if $\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G=\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}$}\\
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Lemma 2

For any well-formed global type
\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
and participants
p,q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
,

(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\varepsilon\}})\{((\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}})/X^{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\}=(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\{\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G/X\})\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\varepsilon\}}.

Proof

By definition.
The starting location
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}
for replacing each
X^{\mathbb{L}}
is correct on the right-hand-side, because
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}
is the location of the recursive call.
Hence, the projection of the unfolded global type will at those spots start with
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}
.

∎

Lemma 3

For any well-formed global type
\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
, participants
p,q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
, and set of locations
\mathbb{L}
,

\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}})=\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}.

Proof

By
definitions

19
,
1
and
2
:

\displaystyle\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{d:relativeProjectionWithLocs}}}{=}

\displaystyle\unfold_{1}(\mu X^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}))

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{d:unfoldRT}}}{=}

\displaystyle\prepend(\mathbb{L},\remPref(\mathbb{L},\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}})\{\prepend(\mathbb{L}^{\prime},\remPref(\mathbb{L},\mu X^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}})))/X^{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{d:relativeProjectionWithLocs}}}{=}

\displaystyle\prepend(\mathbb{L},\remPref(\mathbb{L},\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}})\{\prepend(\mathbb{L}^{\prime},\remPref(\mathbb{L},\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}))/X^{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{l:prefProjection}}}{=}

\displaystyle\prepend(\mathbb{L},(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\varepsilon\}})\{\prepend(\mathbb{L}^{\prime},\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\varepsilon\}})/X^{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{l:prefProjection}}}{=}

\displaystyle\prepend(\mathbb{L},(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\varepsilon\}})\{(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}})/X^{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{l:unfoldRecursiveGTRTnoLabel}}}{=}

\displaystyle\prepend(\mathbb{L},\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\{\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G/X\}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\varepsilon\}})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{l:prefProjection}}}{=}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\{\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G/X\}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{d:unfoldGT}}}{=}

\displaystyle\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}

∎

Lemma 4

For any well-formed global type
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
, participants
p,q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
, and set of locations
\mathbb{L}
,

\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}})=\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}.

Proof

By induction on the number of recursive definitions that
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
starts with (finite by
Definition 5
(Well-formedness)
).
In the base case, the thesis follows trivially.
In the inductive case,
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G=\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\unfold(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{d:relativeProjectionWithLocs}}}{=}

\displaystyle\unfold(\mu X^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}))

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{d:unfoldRT}}}{=}

\displaystyle\unfold_{1}(\mu X^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}))

\displaystyle\overset{\text{IH}}{=}

\displaystyle\unfold_{1}(\mu X^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.(\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime})\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}))

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{d:relativeProjectionWithLocs}}}{=}

\displaystyle\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime})\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{l:unfoldRecursiveGTRT}}}{=}

\displaystyle\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}))\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{d:unfoldGT}}}{=}

\displaystyle\unfold(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime})\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}

∎

Definition 21
(Unfold Monitor)

Given a monitor
\mu X\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M
, we define its
one-level unfolding
:
\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M):=\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\{\mu X\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M/X\}
.
Given a monitor
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M
, we define its
(full) unfolding
, denoted
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M)
, as follows:

\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M):=\begin{cases}\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M))&\text{if $\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\mu X\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}$}\\
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Lemma 5

Suppose given a well-formed global type
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G=\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}
, a participant
p
, and a set of locations
\mathbb{L}
.
Let
D:=\{q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{p\}\mid\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}\neq\mathsf{end}\}
.
Then

\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime},p,D))=\gtToMon(\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}),p,D).

Proof

By definition.
The recursive calls in
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}
concur with the recursive calls in
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}
, and are prefixed by all the exchanges in
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}
in which
p
is involved.

∎

Lemma 6

Suppose given

•

a well-formed global type
\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
,

•

a set of participants
D
,

•

a participant
p\notin D
, and

•

a set of locations
\mathbb{L}
.

Let
E_{1}:=\{q\in D\mid(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}\neq\mathsf{end}\}
and
E_{2}:=\{q\in D\mid(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\{\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G/X\})\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}\neq\mathsf{end}\}
.

Then
E_{1}=E_{2}
.

Proof

For any
q\in E_{1}
,
(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}\neq\mathsf{end}
.
Then, by definition,
G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}\neq\mathsf{end}
.
Hence, the projection of the unfolding of
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
is also not
\mathsf{end}
, and thus
q\in E_{2}
.

For any
q\in E_{2}
, the projection of the unfolding of
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
is not
\mathsf{end}
.
Then, by definition,
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}\neq\mathsf{end}
.
Hence, by definition,
(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}\neq\mathsf{end}
, and thus
q\in E_{1}
.

∎

Lemma 7

Suppose given

•

a well-formed global type
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
,

•

a set of participants
D
,

•

a participant
p\notin D
, and

•

a set of locations
\mathbb{L}
.

Let
D^{\prime}:=\{q\in D\mid\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}}\neq\mathsf{end}\}
.

Then
\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,D)=\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,D^{\prime})
.

Proof

By definition.
Since
p
does not interact with any
q\in D\setminus D^{\prime}
, only the
q\in D^{\prime}
affect the creation of the monitor.

∎

Lemma 8

Suppose given

•

a well-formed global type
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
,

•

a participant
p
, and

•

a set of locations
\mathbb{L}
.

Let
D:=\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{p\}
.

Then
\unfold(\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,D))=\gtToMon(\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G),p,D)
.

Proof

By induction on the number of recursive definitions that
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
starts with (finite by
Definition 5
(Well-formedness)
).
In the base case, the thesis follows trivially.
In the inductive case,
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G=\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}
.
Let
D^{\prime}:=\{q\in D\mid\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\varepsilon}\neq\mathsf{end}\}
.

\displaystyle\unfold(\gtToMon(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime},p,D))

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{d:gtToMon}}}{=}

\displaystyle\unfold(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime},p,D^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{d:unfoldMon}}}{=}

\displaystyle\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\unfold(\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime},p,D^{\prime})))

\displaystyle\overset{\text{IH}}{=}

\displaystyle\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\gtToMon(\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}),p,D^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{l:monitorUnfoldOne}}}{=}

\displaystyle\gtToMon(\unfold_{1}(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime})),p,D^{\prime})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{d:unfoldGT}}}{=}

\displaystyle\gtToMon(\unfold(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}),p,D^{\prime})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypeplural\lx@tilde refnum}{l:activeUnfold} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{l:monitorDropInactive}}}{=}

\displaystyle\gtToMon(\unfold(\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}),p,D)

∎

Lemma 9

Suppose given

•

a well-formed global type
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G=\mu X\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}
,

•

a set of participants
D
, and

•

a participant
p\notin D
.

If
\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,D)=\mathsf{end}
, then
\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime},p,D)\{\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime},p,D)/X\}=\mathsf{end}
.

Proof

Since
\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,D)=\mathsf{end}
, we have that
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\varepsilon\}}=\mathsf{end}
for every
q\in D
.
It follows by definition that
\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime},p,D)=\mathsf{end}
.
Hence, the unfolding is also
\mathsf{end}
.

∎

8.3
Proof of Soundness

Here we prove
theorem

8.1
, which is a generalized version of
theorem

4.2
(Page
4.2
).
We start with an overview of intermediate results used for the proof:

•

Lemma

10
shows that transitions that do not affect parallel networks are independent, i.e., they can be executed in any order without changing the outcome.

•

Lemma

11
shows that we can empty the
\mathtt{Lbls}
map of a satisfaction relation if the locations of all the relative types in
\mathtt{RTs}
succeed all labels in
\dom(\mathtt{Lbls})
, i.e., no exchanges in relative types in
\mathtt{RTs}
relate to the choices recorded in
\mathtt{Lbls}
.

•

Lemma

12
shows that we can eliminate unions of relative types in the
\mathtt{RTs}
map of a satisfaction relation for participants that do not depend on some exchange, allowing us to specifize those independent relative types to some chosen branch.

•

Definition

22
defines a relation between global type, participants, relative types, monitor, blackbox, and buffer, such that together they are witness to a satisfaction relation.

•

Lemma

13
shows an inductive variant of soundness, given an intermediate global type
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
between the initial
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
and the final
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}
.

•

Theorem

8.1
shows soundness.

Lemma 10
(Independence)

Suppose
\mathcal{P}_{1}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{Q}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{R}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{Q}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{R}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{Q}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
, where
\mathcal{P}_{1}\not\equiv\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}
,
\mathcal{P}_{2}\not\equiv\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{2}
,
\mathcal{Q}\not\equiv\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
, and
\mathcal{R}\not\equiv\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
.
Then

\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}c@{}c@{}}\mathcal{P}_{1}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{P}_{2}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{Q}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{R}&{}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}&\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{P}_{2}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{R}\\
\downarrow\mkern-4.0mu{\scriptstyle\tau}&&\downarrow\mkern-4.0mu{\scriptstyle\tau}\\
\mathcal{P}_{1}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{Q}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}&{}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}&\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}.\end{array}

Proof

By definition of the
Definition 2
(LTS for Networks)
, the
\tau
-transitions of both premises are derived from applications of Transition [
par
] and an application of Transition [
out-buf
] or Transition [
out-mon-buf
].
For example, in the first premise,
\mathcal{P}_{1}
does an output which ends up in the buffer of a (monitored) blackbox in
\mathcal{Q}
, leaving
\mathcal{R}
unchanged.
In the second premise,
\mathcal{P}_{2}
does an output which ends up in a buffer in
\mathcal{R}
, leaving
\mathcal{Q}
unchanged.
Hence, the outputs by
\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}
have completely different senders and recipients.
As a result, in a network with all of
\mathcal{P}_{1}
,
\mathcal{P}_{2}
,
\mathcal{Q}
, and
\mathcal{R}
these exchanges do not influence each other.
The conclusion is that the order of these exchanges does not matter.

∎

Lemma 11

Suppose

•

\vDash^{\mathtt{Lbls}}\mathcal{P}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}\mathbin{@}p
and

•

that, for every
(q,\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)\in\mathtt{RTs}
,
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R\neq\mathsf{end}
implies
\big({\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\bigcup}_{(\mathbb{L},\ell)\in\mathtt{Lbls}}\mathbb{L}\big)<\fstLoc(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)
.

Then
\vDash\mathcal{P}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}\mathbin{@}p
.

Proof

By definition: clearly, if all the relative types are at a location past the recorded choices in
\mathtt{Lbls}
, none of the choices in
\mathtt{Lbls}
will ever be used for satisfaction anymore.

∎

As satisfaction iterates through a collection of relative types obtained from a global type, there will be instances where some relative types are independent of the global type’s initial exchange.
By
Definition 16
(Relative Projection with Locations)
, these relative types are the union of the relative projections of the branches of the exchange.
The following lemma assures that we can drop this union and simply continue with the relative projection of the branch followed by the participants involved in the exchange (sender, recipient, and/or depending participants).

Lemma 12

Suppose given

•

a well-formed global type
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G=s{!}r\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}\{i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{i}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}\}_{i\in I}
, and

•

D\supseteq\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{p\}
.

Let
D^{\prime}:=\{q\in D\mid\{p,q\}\subseteq\{s,r\}\}\cup\{q\in D\mid\{p,q\}\mathbin{\Cap}\{s,r\}\wedge(q\mathbin{\@depsOn}p\mathbin{\@in}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\vee p\mathbin{\@depsOn}q\mathbin{\@in}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\}
.

Suppose
\vDash\mathcal{P}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}\mathbin{@}p
, where
\mathtt{RTs}=\{(q,\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{j}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\vec{\ell},j\}})\mid q\in D^{\prime}\}\cup\{(q,{\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\bigcup}_{i\in I}(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{i}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\vec{\ell},i\}}))\mid q\in D\setminus D^{\prime}\}
for some
j\in I
.
Let
\mathtt{RTs}^{\prime}:=\mathtt{RTs}[q\mapsto\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{j}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\vec{\ell},j\}}]_{q\in D\setminus D^{\prime}}
.

Then
\vDash\mathcal{P}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}^{\prime}\mathbin{@}p
.

Proof

For any
q\in D\setminus D^{\prime}
, for every
i,k\in I
,
\erase(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{i}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\vec{\ell},k\}})=\erase(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{k}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\vec{\ell},k\}})
.
Since the satisfaction holds for an empty label function (i.e.,
\vDash^{\emptyset}\mathcal{P}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}\mathbin{@}p
), the locations formed in each
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{i}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\vec{\ell},i\}}
are insignificant.
Hence, it suffices to simply use
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{j}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\vec{\ell},j\}}
for every
q\in D\setminus D^{\prime}
.

∎

Definition 22
(Initial satisfaction)

We define
initial satisfaction
, denoted
(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,\mathbb{L},D)\mathrel{\Vdash}(\mathtt{RTs},D^{\prime},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M,P,\vec{m})
, to hold if and only if

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle\supseteq\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{p\},

\displaystyle\mathtt{RTs}

\displaystyle=\{(q,\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}})\mid q\in D\},

\displaystyle D^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\{q\in D\mid\unfold\big(\mathtt{RTs}(q)\big)\neq\mathsf{end}\},

\displaystyle\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,D^{\prime})\neq\mathsf{end}

\displaystyle\implies\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,D^{\prime})

\displaystyle\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,D^{\prime})=\mathsf{end}

\displaystyle\implies\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\in\{\mathsf{end},\checkmark\}

\displaystyle\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}\mathbin{@}p

Lemma 13
(Soundness — Generalized)

•

Suppose given well-formed global types
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
and
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
such that
\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\geq 2
and
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\vec{\ell}_{0}$}}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
.

•

For every
p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
,

–

suppose given a process
P_{p}^{0}
and buffer
\vec{m}_{p}^{0}
, and

–

take
\mathtt{RTs}_{p}^{0},D_{p}^{0},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}^{0}
such that

(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0},p,\{\vec{\ell}_{0}\},\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{p\})\mathrel{\Vdash}(\mathtt{RTs}_{p}^{0},D_{p}^{0},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}^{0},P_{p}^{0},\vec{m}_{p}^{0}).

•

Suppose
\mathcal{P}_{0}:={\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\prod}_{p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{p}^{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}_{p}^{0}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}^{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\Rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
.

Then there exist
\vec{\ell}^{\prime},\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}
such that

•

\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\vec{\ell}^{\prime}$}}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}
,

•

for every
p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
there exist
P^{\prime}_{p},\vec{m}^{\prime}_{p},\mathtt{RTs}^{\prime}_{p},D^{\prime}_{p},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}_{p}
such that

(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime},p,\{\vec{\ell}_{0},\vec{\ell}^{\prime}\},\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{p\})\mathrel{\Vdash}(\mathtt{RTs}^{\prime}_{p},D^{\prime}_{p},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}_{p},P^{\prime}_{p},\vec{m}^{\prime}_{p}),

and

•

\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\Rightarrow{\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\prod}_{p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P^{\prime}_{p}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}^{\prime}_{p}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}_{p}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}
.

Proof

By induction on the number of transitions
n
from
\mathcal{P}_{0}
to
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
(IH
1
).

In the base case, where
n=0
, the thesis follows immediately, because
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}_{0}
.
To be precise, the assumption satisfies the conclusion by letting:
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}=\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
;
\vec{\ell}=\varepsilon
; for every
p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
,
P^{\prime}_{p}=P_{p}^{0}
,
\vec{m}^{\prime}_{p}=\vec{m}_{p}^{0}
,
D^{\prime}_{p}=D_{p}^{0}
,
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}_{p}=\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}^{0}
,
\mathtt{RTs}^{\prime}_{p}=\mathtt{RTs}_{p}^{0}
.

In the inductive case, where
n\geq 1
, we use the shape of
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
and the assumed satisfactions of the monitored processes to determine the possible transitions from
\mathcal{P}_{0}
.
We then follow these transitions, and show that we can reach a network
\mathcal{P}_{1}
where all the monitored processes satisfy some
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{1}
with
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\ell_{1}$}}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{1}
.
If at this point we already passed through
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, the thesis is proven.
Otherwise, the thesis follows from IH
1
, because the number of transitions from
\mathcal{P}_{1}
is less than
n
.

There is a subtlety that we should not overlook:
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
may contain several consecutive, independent exchanges.
For example, suppose
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}=p{!}q\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}\{i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.s{!}r\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}\{j\langle T_{j}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{i,j}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}\}_{j\in J}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}\}_{i\in I}
where
\{p,q\}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.33334pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\mathbin{\Cap}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.33334pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\mathbin{\Cap}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.69444pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\mathbin{\Cap}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.36115pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\mathbin{\Cap}}}}\{s,r\}
.
Monitors nor satisfaction can prevent the exchange between
s
and
r
from happening before the exchange between
p
and
q
has been completed.
Hence, the transitions from
\mathcal{P}_{0}
to
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
may not entirely follow the order specified by
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
.

We deal with this issue by applying induction on the number of out-of-order exchanges observed in the transitions to
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
.
We then follow the transitions determined by monitors and satisfaction, in the order specified by
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
, effectively “postponing” the out-of-order exchanges until it is their turn.
We keep doing this, until we have eventually passed through all the postponed out-of-order exchanges.
At this point, we have found an alternative path from
\mathcal{P}_{0}
to the final network.
To reconcile this alternative path with the path from
\mathcal{P}_{0}
to
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, we apply
Lemma 10
(Independence)
.
This lemma essentially states that independent exchanges may be performed in any order, as they do not influence each other.
Hence, we use
Lemma 10
(Independence)
to move the postponed transitions back to their original position in the path from
\mathcal{P}_{0}
to
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, proving the thesis.
Hereafter, we assume independent exchanges dealt with.

As a first step in our analysis, we consider the fact that
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
may start with recursive definitions.
Let
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{1}:=\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})
; by definition,
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{1}
does not start with recursive definitions.
For every
p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
, let
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}^{1}:=\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}^{0})
; by
lemma

8
,
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}^{1}=\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{1},p,D_{p}^{0})
, and, by definition of the
Definition 2
(LTS for Networks)
, the LTS of
{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{p}^{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}_{p}^{0}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}^{1}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}
is equivalent to that of
{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{p}^{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}_{p}^{0}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}^{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}
.
For every
p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
, let
\mathtt{RTs}_{p}^{1}:=\{(q,\unfold(\mathtt{RTs}_{p}^{0}(q)))\mid q\in D_{p}^{0}\}
; by
lemma

4
, for every
q\in D_{p}^{0}
,
\mathtt{RTs}_{p}^{1}(q)=\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{1}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)
.
The conditions for Satisfaction in
fig.

5
unfold any relative types.
Hence, we can reuse the satisfaction given by the original initial satisfaction, to show that
(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{1},p,\{\vec{l}_{0}\},\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{p\})\mathrel{\Vdash}(\mathtt{RTs}_{p}^{1},D_{p}^{0},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}^{1},P_{p}^{0},\vec{m}_{p}^{0})
.
We then continue our analysis from this new unfolded initial satisfaction, for which all results transfer back to the original initial satisfaction.

The rest of our analysis depends on the shape of
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{1}
(exchange or end).

•

Exchange:
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{1}=s{!}r\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}\{i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{i}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}\}_{i\in I}
.

Let us make an inventory of all relative types and monitors at this point.
We use this information to determine the possible behavior of the monitored blackboxes, and their interactions.
This behavior, in combination with satisfaction, allows us to determine exactly how the network evolves and reaches a state required to apply IH
1
.

–

For
s
we have:

\displaystyle\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{1}(r)

\displaystyle=s{!}r^{\{\vec{\ell}_{0}\}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{i}\mathbin{\rangle}(s,r)^{\{\vec{\ell}_{0},i\}})\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}

\displaystyle\mathsf{deps}_{s}

\displaystyle=\{q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\mid q\mathbin{\@depsOn}s\mathbin{\@in}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{1}\}

\displaystyle\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{1}(q)

\displaystyle=(s{!}r){!}q^{\{\vec{\ell}_{0}\}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{i}\mathbin{\rangle}(s,q)^{\{\vec{\ell}_{0},i\}})\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}\quad[q\in\mathsf{deps}_{s}]

\displaystyle\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{1}(q)

\displaystyle={\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\bigcup}_{i\in I}(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{i}\mathbin{\rangle}(s,q)^{\{\vec{\ell}_{0},i\}})\quad[q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{s,r\}\setminus\mathsf{deps}_{s}]

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{s}^{1}

\displaystyle=s{!}r\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.s{!}\mathsf{deps}_{s}(i)\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{i},s,D_{s}^{0})\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{i\in I}

Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
(
Output
) allows the monitored blackbox of
s
to send
j\langle T_{j}\rangle
for any
j\in I
to
r
.
Then
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
(
Dependency Output
) allows the monitored blackbox to send
j
to all
q\in\mathsf{deps}_{s}
(concurrently).
However, there may be other relative types in
\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{1}
that allow/require the monitored blackbox of to perform other tasks.

Since the outputs above precede any other communications in
\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{1}
(they originate from the first exchange in
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{1}
), by the progress property of
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
, the monitored blackbox will keep transitioning.
The monitor
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{s}^{1}
requires the blackbox to first perform the output above, and then the dependency outputs above.
The buffered blackbox does not take any transitions other than
\tau
and the outputs above: otherwise, the monitor would transition to an error signal, which cannot transition, contradicting the satisfaction’s progress property.

It then follows that the monitored blackbox sends
j\langle T_{j}\rangle
to
r
for some
j\in I
, after which it sends
j
to each
q\in\mathsf{deps}_{s}
(in any order), possibly interleaved with
\tau
-transitions from the blackbox (finitely many, by
Definition 1
(Assumptions)
(
Finite
\tau
)) or from the buffered blackbox reading messages; let
P_{s}^{2}
and
\vec{m}_{s}^{2}
be the resulting blackbox and buffer, respectively.
By
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
, we have the following:

\displaystyle\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{2}

\displaystyle:=\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{1}[q\mapsto\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{j}\mathbin{\rangle}(s,q)^{\{\vec{\ell}_{0},j\}}]_{q\in\{r\}\cup\mathsf{deps}_{s}}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{s}^{2}

\displaystyle:=\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{j},s,D_{s}^{0})

\displaystyle\vDash^{\{(\{\vec{\ell}_{0}\},j)\}}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}s\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{s}^{2}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}_{s}^{2}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{s}^{2}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{2}\mathbin{@}s

Clearly, for each
q\in\dom(\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{2})
,
\vec{\ell}_{0}\leq\fstLoc(\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{2}(q))
.
Hence, by
lemma

11
,
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}s\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{s}^{2}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}_{s}^{2}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{s}^{2}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{2}\mathbin{@}s
.
Let
\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{3}:=\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{2}[q\mapsto\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{j}\mathbin{\rangle}(r,q)^{\{\vec{\ell}_{0},j\}}]_{q\in D\setminus\mathsf{deps}_{s}\setminus\{s,r\}}
.
Then, by
lemma

12
,
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}s\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{s}^{2}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}_{s}^{2}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{s}^{2}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{3}\mathbin{@}s
.
Let
D_{s}^{3}:=\{q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{s\}\mid\unfold\big(\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{3}(q)\big)\neq\mathsf{end}\}
and

.
By

,
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{s}^{2}=\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{s}^{3}
.
We have
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{1}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$j$}}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{j}
.
In conclusion,

(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{j},s,\{\vec{\ell}_{0},j\},\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{s\})\mathrel{\Vdash}(\mathtt{RTs}_{s}^{3},D_{s}^{3},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{s}^{3},P_{s}^{2},\vec{m}_{s}^{2}),

such that the premise of IH
1
is satisfied for

.

–

For
r
we have:

\displaystyle\mathtt{RTs}_{r}^{1}(s)

\displaystyle=s{!}r^{\{\vec{\ell}_{0}\}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{i}\mathbin{\rangle}(r,s)^{\{\vec{\ell}_{0},i\}})\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}

\displaystyle\mathsf{deps}_{r}

\displaystyle=\{q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\mid q\mathbin{\@depsOn}r\mathbin{\@in}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{1}\}

\displaystyle\mathtt{RTs}_{r}^{1}(q)

\displaystyle=(r{?}s){!}q^{\{\vec{\ell}_{0}\}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{i}\mathbin{\rangle}(r,q)^{\{\vec{\ell}_{0},i\}})\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}\quad[q\in\mathsf{deps}_{r}]

\displaystyle={\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\bigcup}_{i\in I}(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{i}\mathbin{\rangle}(r,q)^{\{\vec{\ell}_{0},i\}})\quad[q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{s,r\}\setminus\mathsf{deps}_{r}]

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{r}^{1}

\displaystyle=r{?}s\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.r{!}\mathsf{deps}_{r}(i)\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{i},r,D_{r}^{0})\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{i\in I}

Since

to

], this message will end up in the buffer of the monitored blackbox of

\displaystyle D_{r}^{2}

\displaystyle={\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\bigcup}_{i\in I}(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{i}\mathbin{\rangle}(q,q^{\prime})^{\{\vec{\ell}_{0}\}+i})\quad[q^{\prime}\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{q,s,r\}]

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{q}^{1}

\displaystyle=q{?}s\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\big\{\mskip-5.0mu\big\{i\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.q{?}r\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{i\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{i},q,D_{q}^{0})\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}\cup\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{j\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\mathsf{error}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{j\in I\setminus\{i\}}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\big\}\mskip-5.0mu\big\}_{i\in I}

\displaystyle\mathtt{RTs}_{q}^{1}(q^{\prime})

\displaystyle={\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\bigcup}_{i\in I}(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}H_{i}\mathbin{\rangle}(q,q^{\prime})^{\{\vec{\ell}_{0}\}+i})\quad[q^{\prime}\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{q\}]

is satisfied.

1
.

•

End:
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}=\mathsf{end}
.

For every
p\neq q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
, we have
\mathtt{RTs}_{p}^{1}(q)=\mathsf{end}
.
By the definition of
Definition 22
(Initial satisfaction)
, for each
p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
,
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}^{1}\in\{\mathsf{end},\checkmark\}
.
By the progress properties of
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
, each monitored blackbox will continue performing transitions; these can only be
\tau
-transitions, for any other transition leads to an error signal or a violation of Satisfaction.
That is, each monitored blackbox will be reading messages from buffers or doing internal computations.
However, there are only finitely many messages, and, by
Definition 1
(Assumptions)
(
Finite
\tau
), each blackbox is assumed to only perform finitely many
\tau
-transitions in a row.
Hence, at some point, we must see an
\mathsf{end}
-transition from each monitored blackbox.

Suppose the observed transition originates from
p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
.
Suppose
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}^{1}=\mathsf{end}
.
Then the transition is either labeled
\tau
or
\mathsf{end}
.
If the label is
\tau
, IH
1
applies with premise trivially satisfied.
If the label is
\mathsf{end}
, we follow
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
(
End
) to apply IH
1
.

It cannot be that
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}^{1}=\checkmark
: the
Definition 2
(LTS for Networks)
does not define any transitions, contradicting the observed transition.
∎

Theorem 8.1
(Soundness)

•

Suppose given a well-formed global type
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
.

•

For every
p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
, suppose given a process
P_{p}
, and take
\mathtt{RTs}_{p},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}
such that

(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,\{\varepsilon\},\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{p\})\mathrel{\Vdash}(\mathtt{RTs}_{p},\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{p\},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p},P_{p},\varepsilon).

•

Suppose also
{\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\prod}_{p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{p}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\Rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
.

Then there exist
\vec{\ell}^{\prime},\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}
such that

•

\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\vec{\ell}^{\prime}$}}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}
,

•

for every
p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)
there exist
P^{\prime}_{p},\vec{m}^{\prime}_{p},\mathtt{RTs}^{\prime}_{p},D^{\prime}_{p},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}_{p}
such that

–

(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime},p,\{\ell^{\prime}\},\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{p\})\mathrel{\Vdash}(\mathtt{RTs}^{\prime}_{p},D^{\prime}_{p},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}_{p},P^{\prime}_{p},\vec{m}^{\prime}_{p})
, and

–

\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\Rightarrow{\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\prod}_{p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P^{\prime}_{p}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}^{\prime}_{p}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}_{p}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}
.

Proof

Follows directly from
lemma

13
, given
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\varepsilon$}}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
.

∎

Theorem 8.2
(Error Freedom)

Suppose given a well-formed global type
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
.
For every
p\in\prt(G)
, suppose given a process
P_{p}
, and take
\mathtt{RTs}_{p},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}
such that
(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,\{\varepsilon\},\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{p\})\mathrel{\Vdash}(\mathtt{RTs}_{p},\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G)\setminus\{p\},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p},P_{p},\varepsilon)
.
If
\mathcal{P}:={\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\prod}_{p\in P}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{p}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{p}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\Rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, then there are no
\mathcal{Q},D
such that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\mathcal{Q}\mathbin{|}\mathsf{error}_{D}
.

Proof

Suppose, towards a contradiction, that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\mathcal{Q}\mathbin{|}\mathsf{error}_{D}
.
Then, by multiple applications of Transition [
error-par
],
\mathcal{Q}\mathbin{|}\mathsf{error}_{D}\Rightarrow\mathsf{error}_{D^{\prime}}
for some
D^{\prime}
.
Hence,
\mathcal{P}\Rightarrow\mathsf{error}_{D^{\prime}}
.
Then, by
Theorem 8.1
(Soundness)
,
\mathsf{error}_{D^{\prime}}
would further transition.
However, the LTS of networks does not specify any transitions for error signals: a contradiction.

∎

8.4
Proof of Transparency

Here we prove
theorem

8.3
, which is the full version of
theorem

4.4
(
theorem

4.4
).
We start with an overview of intermediate results used for the proof:

•

Definition

23
defines an LTS for relative types, where transitions can only be (dependency) outputs.

•

Definition

24
defines a relation between a global type, participant, monitor,
\mathtt{RTs}
, and buffer.
The idea is that the relative types in
\mathtt{RTs}
may be in an intermediate state between exchanges in the global type, reflected by messages in the buffer.

•

Lemma

14
shows that monitored blackboxes in a satisfaction relation do not transition to an error state through the
Definition 11
(Enhanced LTS for Networks)
(
definition

11
).

•

Lemma

15
shows that messages in the buffer of a monitored blackbox of
p
in a satisfaction relation are related to exchanges to
p
in
\mathtt{RTs}
of the satisfaction relation.

•

Lemma

16
shows that an output transitions of a monitored blackbox in a satisfaction relation implies that the monitor is a related output, possibly preceded by a dependency output.

•

Lemma

17
shows that an
\mathsf{end}
-transition of a monitored blackbox in a satisfaction relation implies that the monitor is
\mathsf{end}
, possibly preceded by a dependency output.

•

Lemma

18
shows that a message in the buffer of a monitored blackbox relates to an exchange in a global type, reachable in finitely many steps.

•

Lemma

19
shows that if the blackbox of a monitored blackbox in a satisfaction relation does an input transition, then the monitor is a sequence of inputs ending in an input related to the input transition.

•

Lemma

20
shows that if the blackbox of a monitored blackbox in a satisfaction relation does a
\tau
-transition, then the monitor is not
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\checkmark
.

•

Lemma

21
shows that label oracles are equal for relative types prior and past dependency outputs.

•

Lemma

22
shows a precise relation between actions and label oracles.

•

Theorem

8.3
shows transparency.

Definition 23
(LTS for Relative Types)

We define an LTS for relative types, denoted
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$m$}}_{p}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R^{\prime}
, where actions are output (dependency) messages
m
(
fig.

2
(top)) and
p
is the receiving participant, by the following rules:

\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 14.41666pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 52.8523pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$j\in I$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to135.79773pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$q{!}p^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$q{!}p(j\langle T_{j}\rangle)$}}_{p}$}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{j}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 14.41666pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 46.43095pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$j\in I$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to122.95506pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$(q\lozenge r){!}p^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}\mathrel{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$q{!}p(\mskip-4.0mu(j)\mskip-4.0mu)$}}_{p}$}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{j}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 15.20613pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\unfold(\mu X^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$m$}}_{p}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R^{\prime}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to108.09595pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 19.35483pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mu X^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$m$}}_{p}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R^{\prime}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

Given
\vec{m}=m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}
, we write
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\vec{m}$}}_{p}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R^{\prime}
to denote
\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$m_{1}$}}_{p}\ldots\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$m_{k}$}}_{p}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R^{\prime}
(reflexive if
\vec{m}=\varepsilon
).
We write
\vec{m}(q)
to denote the subsequence of messages from
\vec{m}
sent by
q
.

Definition 24
(Coherent Setup (
\bowtie
))

A global type
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
, a participant
p
, a monitor
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\neq\mu X\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}
, a map
\mathtt{RTs}:\bm{P}\rightarrow\bm{R}
, and a sequence of messages
\vec{n}
are
in a coherent setup
, denoted
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}p\mathrel{\bowtie}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathrel{\bowtie}\mathtt{RTs}\mathrel{\bowtie}\vec{n}
, if and only if there exist
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,\vec{\ell}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\vec{\ell}$}}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
,
\vec{n}
exclusively contains messages with sender in
\dom(\mathtt{RTs})
, and

•

(Initial state)

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\in\{\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})\setminus\{p\}),\checkmark\}
implies that

–

for every
q\in\dom(\mathtt{RTs})
there exists
\mathbb{L}_{q}
such that
(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}_{q}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\vec{n}(q)$}}_{p}\mathtt{RTs}(q)
, and

–

if
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\checkmark
then
\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})\setminus\{p\})=\mathsf{end}
;

•

(Intermediate state)

Otherwise, there exist
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime},j
such that
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$j$}}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}
, and either of the following holds:

–

all of the following hold:

*

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=p{!}D(j)\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime},p,\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})\setminus\{p\})
,

*

for every
q\in\dom(\mathtt{RTs})\setminus D
there exists
\mathbb{L}_{q}
such that
(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}_{q}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\vec{n}(q)$}}_{p}\mathtt{RTs}(q)
, and

*

for every
q\in D
there exists
\mathbb{L}_{q}
such that
\mathtt{RTs}(q)=\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}_{q}}
;

–

all of the following hold:

*

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=p{?}r\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{j\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime},p,\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})\setminus\{p\})\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}\cup\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{i\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\mathsf{error}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{i\in I\setminus\{j\}}
,

*

for every
q\in\dom(\mathtt{RTs})\setminus\{r\}
there exists
\mathbb{L}_{q}
such that
(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}_{q}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\vec{n}(q)$}}_{p}\mathtt{RTs}(q)
, and

*

there exists
\mathbb{L}_{r}
such that
(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\mathbin{\rangle}(p,r)^{\mathbb{L}_{r}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\vec{n}(r)$}}_{p}\mathtt{RTs}(r)
.

Lemma 14

Suppose

•

\mathcal{R}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}_{0}\mathbin{@}p
,

•

where
\mathtt{RTs}_{0}:=\{(q,\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\varepsilon\}})\mid q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})\setminus\{p\}\}
for well-formed
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
.

Then

•

for any
({\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]},\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})\in\mathcal{R}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}p\mathrel{\bowtie}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathrel{\bowtie}\mathtt{RTs}\mathrel{\bowtie}\vec{n}
, and

•

for any
\alpha,\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\Omega,\Omega^{\prime}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\mathrel{{}_{\Omega}{\mathrel{\hbox to12.89pt{\vbox to12.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.4438pt\lower-7.75134pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{

\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}_{\Omega^{\prime}}}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
,

we have
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\not\equiv\mathsf{error}_{\{p\}}
.

Proof

Suppose, towards a contradiction, that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\equiv\mathsf{error}_{\{p\}}
.
Then the transition is due to Transition [
no-dep
],
\alpha=\tau
,
\Omega^{\prime}=\Omega
, and the transition is derived from Transition [
error-out
], [
error-in
], or [
error-mon
].
We show in each case separately that this leads to a contradiction.

•

Transition [
error-out
].
Then
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M
denotes an output, and so, by the definition of
Definition 24
(Coherent Setup (
\bowtie
))
, there is a
q\in\dom(\mathtt{RTs})
such that
\unfold\big(\mathtt{RTs}(q)\big)
is an output with a location that prefixes all other locations in
\mathtt{RTs}
.
By
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
(
Tau
),
(\mathsf{error}_{\{p\}},\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})\in\mathcal{R}
.
Then by the progress property of
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
, there exist
\alpha,\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathsf{error}_{\{p\}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\alpha$}}\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}
.
However, the
Definition 2
(LTS for Networks)
does not define such a transition: a contradiction.

•

Transition [
error-in
].
Then the monitor tries to read a message from
\vec{n}
, but the first relevant message is incorrect.
By the definition of
Definition 24
(Coherent Setup (
\bowtie
))
, all messages in
\vec{n}
are in accordance with
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
(
Input
) or (
Dependency input
).
But then the message cannot be incorrect: a contradiction.

•

Transition [
error-mon
].
This means that
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\mathsf{error}
.
This can only be the case after two related dependency inputs of different labels.
However, the messages read must be due to
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
(
Dependency input
), where the first one records the chosen label in
\mathtt{Lbls}
, and the second one uses that label.
Hence, it is not possible that two different labels have been received: a contradiction.

Hence,
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\not\equiv\mathsf{error}_{\{p\}}
.

∎

Lemma 15

Suppose

•

\mathcal{R}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}_{0}\mathbin{@}p
,

•

where
\mathtt{RTs}_{0}:=\{(q,\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\varepsilon\}})\mid q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})\setminus\{p\}\}

•

for well-formed
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
with
p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})
.

Then

•

for any
({\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]},\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})\in\mathcal{R}

•

with non-empty
\vec{n}

•

such that
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}p\mathrel{\bowtie}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathrel{\bowtie}\mathtt{RTs}\mathrel{\bowtie}\vec{n}
,

there exist

•

\mathtt{RTs}^{\prime},\mathtt{Lbls}^{\prime}
such that
({\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]},\mathtt{RTs}^{\prime},\mathtt{Lbls}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}
, and

•

(q,\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)\in\dom(\mathtt{RTs}^{\prime})
such that

–

\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)=q{!}p^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}

–

or
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)=(q\lozenge r){!}p^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}
.

Proof

Take any
({\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]},\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})\in\mathcal{R}
with non-empty
\vec{n}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}p\mathrel{\bowtie}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathrel{\bowtie}\mathtt{RTs}\mathrel{\bowtie}\vec{n}
.
By the minimality of
\mathcal{R}
, each message in
\vec{n}
traced back to applications of
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
(
Input
) or (
Dependency input
).
By the definition of
Definition 24
(Coherent Setup (
\bowtie
))
, each relative type in
\mathtt{RTs}
relates
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
with the messages in
\vec{n}
through the
Definition 23
(LTS for Relative Types)
.
Hence, at
({\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]},\mathtt{RTs}^{\prime},\mathtt{Lbls}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}
, since
\vec{n}
is non-empty, the unfolding of at least one relative type in
\mathtt{RTs}^{\prime}
denotes an input by
p
.

∎

Lemma 16

•

Suppose

–

\mathcal{R}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}_{0}\mathbin{@}p
,

–

where
\mathtt{RTs}_{0}:=\{(q,\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\varepsilon\}})\mid q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})\setminus\{p\}\}

for well-formed
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}

({\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]},\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})\in\mathcal{R}

\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}p\mathrel{\bowtie}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathrel{\bowtie}\mathtt{RTs}\mathrel{\bowtie}\vec{n}
.

•

Suppose
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$p{!}q(j\langle T_{j}\rangle)$}}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P^{\prime}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}
.

Then

•

\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M)=\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}

•

or
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M)=p{!}D(\ell)\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}

where
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}=p{!}q\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}_{i}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{i\in I}
s.t.
j\in I
.

Proof

Suppose, toward a contradiction, that
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M)\neq\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}
and
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M)\neq p{!}D(\ell)\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}
.
Then, by Transition [
error-out
],
{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}\mathsf{error}_{\{p\}}
.
By
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
(
Tau
),
(\mathsf{error}_{\{p\}},\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})\in\mathcal{R}
.
If
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M
starts with a sequence of recursive definitions, we unfold them; by Transition [
mon-rec
], the behavior is unchanged; let us assume, w.l.o.g., that
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M
does not start with a recursive definition.
From each possible shape of
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M
, we derive a contradiction.

•

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=p{!}r\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{k\langle T_{k}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{k}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{k\in K}
for
r\neq q
.
By definition of
Definition 24
(Coherent Setup (
\bowtie
))
,
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\vec{\ell}$}}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
,
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})\setminus\{p\})
, and there exists
\mathbb{L}
such that

(LTS for Relative Types)
only allows input transitions, then

Clearly,

prefixes all other locations in
\mathtt{RTs}
.
Hence, by the progress property of

(Satisfaction)
, there exists
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
such that
\mathsf{error}_{\{p\}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
.
However, the
Definition 2
(LTS for Networks)
does not define such a transition: a contradiction.

•

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=p{!}q\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{i}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{i\in I}
for
j\notin I
.
Analogous to the case above.

•

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=p{?}r\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{k\langle T_{k}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{k}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{k\in K}

(Coherent Setup (

,
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G,p,\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})\setminus\{p\})
, and there exists
\mathbb{L}
such that

({\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]},\mathtt{RTs}^{\prime},\mathtt{Lbls}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}

(

: a contradiction.

•

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=p{?}r\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{k\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{k}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{k\in K}
.
Analogous to the case above.

•

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\mathsf{end}
.

Definition 24
(Coherent Setup (
\bowtie
))
,
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\vec{\ell}$}}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G
,

, and for every

only allows input transitions, for every

(Satisfaction)
(

), there exists

(LTS for Networks)
does not define such transitions: a contradiction.

•

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\mathsf{error}
.

(Coherent Setup (
\bowtie
))
,
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\neq\mathsf{error}

•

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\checkmark

must have been reached through
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
(
End
).
Then
{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\checkmark\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.5pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\rightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.5pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\rightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.04861pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\rightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.84029pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\rightarrow}}}
: a contradiction.
