Lemma 17

•

Suppose

–

\mathcal{R}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}_{0}\mathbin{@}p
,

–

where
\mathtt{RTs}_{0}:=\{(q,\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\varepsilon\}})\mid q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})\setminus\{p\}\}

–

for well-formed
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
with
p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})
.

•

Take any
({\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]},\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})\in\mathcal{R}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}p\mathrel{\bowtie}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathrel{\bowtie}\mathtt{RTs}\mathrel{\bowtie}\vec{n}
.

•

Suppose
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\mathsf{end}$}}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P^{\prime}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}
.

Then
\vec{n}=\varepsilon
, and

•

\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M)=\mathsf{end}

•

or
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M)=p{!}D(\ell)\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\mathsf{end}
.

Proof

Suppose toward a contradiction that
\vec{n}\neq\varepsilon
or that
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M)\notin\{\mathsf{end},p{!}D(\ell)\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\mathsf{end}\}
.
Then, by Transition [
error-end
],
{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}\mathsf{error}_{\{p\}}
.
The contradiction follows similar to the reasoning in the proof of
lemma

16
.
We only discuss the additional case where
\vec{n}\neq\varepsilon
and
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=p{!}D(\ell)\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}
.
By induction on the size of
D
,
{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}
.
By the definition of
Definition 24
(Coherent Setup (
\bowtie
))
,
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M
is synthesized from a global type, so
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}
is not a dependency output (there are never two consecutive dependency outputs).
Hence, the other cases for
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}
apply and the search for a contradiction continues as usual.

∎

Lemma 18

Suppose

•

\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}p\mathrel{\bowtie}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathrel{\bowtie}\mathtt{RTs}\mathrel{\bowtie}\vec{n},u

•

with
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\vec{\ell}$}}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$k$}}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}

•

and
u=q{!}p(j\langle T_{j}\rangle)
.

Then

•

\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}=q{!}p\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}\{i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}_{i}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}\}_{i\in I}
with
j\in I

•

or, for every
k^{\prime},\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime\prime}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$k^{\prime}$}}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime\prime}
, there exists (finite)
\vec{\ell}^{\prime}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\vec{\ell}^{\prime}$}}q{!}p\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}\{i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}.\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime\prime}_{i}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}\}_{i\in I}
with
j\in I
.

Proof

By the definition of
Definition 24
(Coherent Setup (
\bowtie
))
, there are
\mathbb{L},\mathbb{L}^{\prime}
such that
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}})=q{!}p^{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}
with
j\in I
.
By definition, this relative type is generated in finitely many steps by
algorithm

3
(
algorithm

3
).
We apply induction on the number
x
of steps this took.
In the base case, the thesis holds trivially.

In the inductive case, the relative projections of all branches of
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}
onto
(p,q)
are the same.
Take any
k^{\prime},\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime\prime}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$k^{\prime}$}}\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime\prime}
.
Then
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\mathbb{L}+k^{\prime}}
is generated in less than
x
steps through
algorithm

3
(
algorithm

3
).
Hence, the thesis follows from the IH.

∎

Lemma 19

•

•

and
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M)\neq p{?}q\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{i}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{i\in I}
for
I\supseteq\{j\}
,

then
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M)\in\{p{?}r\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{k\langle T_{k}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{k}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{k\in K},p{?}r\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{k\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{k}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{k\in K},p{!}D(\ell)\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}\mid r\neq q\}
.

Proof

Assume, toward a contradiction, that
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M)\notin\{p{?}r\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{k\langle T_{k}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{k}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{k\in K},p{?}r\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{k\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{k}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{k\in K},p{!}D(\ell)\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M^{\prime}\mid r\neq q\}
.
We discuss each possible shape of
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M)
separetely (w.l.o.g., assume
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M
does not start with a recursive definition, i.e.,
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M)=\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M
).

•

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=p{!}r\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{k\langle T_{k}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{k}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{k\in K}
.
By definition of
Definition 24
(Coherent Setup (
\bowtie
))
,
\unfold\big(\mathtt{RTs}(r)\big)=p{!}r^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace k\langle T_{k}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{k}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{k\in K}
for some
\mathbb{L}
.
It must then be that
\mathbb{L}
is the earliest location in all
\mathtt{RTs}
.
Then, by the progress property of
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
, the monitored blackbox must eventually do an output transition.
However, by
Definition 1
(Assumptions)
(
Input/Output
), since
P
does an input transition, it cannot do an output transition: a contradiction.

•

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=p{?}q\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\{\mskip-5.0mu\{i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{i}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}\}\mskip-5.0mu\}_{i\in I}
for
j\notin I
.
Analogous to the similar case in the proof of
lemma

16
.

•

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\mathsf{end}
.
Analogous to the similar case in the proof of
lemma

16
.

•

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\mathsf{error}
.
Analogous to the similar case in the proof of
lemma

16
.

•

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\checkmark
.
It cannot be the case the
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0}=\checkmark
, and, by the
Definition 2
(LTS for Networks)
,
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\checkmark
can only be reached through a transition labeled
\mathsf{end}
.
Hence, the current monitored blackbox must have been reached through
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
(
End
) from
{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\mathsf{end}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}
.
An
\mathsf{end}
-transition from this state requires that
\vec{n}
is empty, which it is not: a contradiction.

Hence, the thesis must hold indeed.

∎

Lemma 20

•

Suppose

–

\mathcal{R}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}_{0}\mathbin{@}p
,

–

where
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}p\mathrel{\bowtie}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}\mathtt{RTs}_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}\varepsilon

–

for well-formed
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
with
p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})
.

•

Take any
\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls},\vec{m},\vec{n},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M,P
such that

–

({\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]},\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})\in\mathcal{R}
,
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}p\mathrel{\bowtie}M\mathrel{\bowtie}\mathtt{RTs}\mathrel{\bowtie}\vec{n}
,

–

and
\vec{n}
is non-empty.

If
P\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}P^{\prime}
, then
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\neq\checkmark
.

Proof

Suppose, toward a contradiction, that
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\checkmark
.
We have
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0}\neq\checkmark
, so this state must have been reached through an
\mathsf{end}
-transition between
P_{0}
and
P
.
However, by
Definition 1
(Assumptions)
(
End
), there can be no transitions after an
\mathsf{end}
-transition: a contradiction.

∎

Lemma 21

Suppose given
\mathtt{RTs}:\bm{P}\rightarrow\bm{R}
and
\mathtt{Lbls}:\Pow(\vec{\bm{L}})\rightarrow\bm{L}
.

If there is
(q,\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)\in\mathtt{RTs}
such that
\unfold(\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)=(p\lozenge r){!}q^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}
, then, for every
j\in I
,

\LO(p,\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})=\LO(p,\mathtt{RTs}[q\mapsto\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{j}],\mathtt{Lbls}).

Proof

Trivally, by the definition of
Definition 13
(Label Oracle)
, the right-hand-side is a subset of the left-hand-side.
For the other direction, the update of
\mathtt{RTs}
might add additional sequences.
However, since the update to
\mathtt{RTs}
does not affect any other relative types and
\mathtt{Lbls}
is not updated, no sequences are removed.
Hence, the left-hand-side is a subset of the right-hand-side.

∎

Lemma 22

Suppose given
\mathtt{RTs}:\bm{P}\rightarrow\bm{R}
,
\mathtt{Lbls}:\Pow(\vec{\bm{L}})\rightarrow\bm{L}
,
\Omega=\LO(p,\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})
, and
\Omega^{\prime}=\Omega(\alpha)
.

Then all of the following hold:

•

If
\alpha=p{!}q(j\langle T_{j}\rangle)
, then
\mathtt{RTs}(q)\mathrel{\circeq}p{!}q^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}
with
j\in I
, and
\Omega^{\prime}=\LO(p,\mathtt{RTs}[q\mapsto\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{j}],\mathtt{Lbls}[\mathbb{L}\mapsto j])
.

•

If
\alpha=p{?}q(j\langle T_{j}\rangle)
, then
\mathtt{RTs}(q)\mathrel{\circeq}q{!}p^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\langle T_{i}\rangle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}
with
j\in I
, and
\Omega^{\prime}=\LO(p,\mathtt{RTs}[q\mapsto\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{j}],\mathtt{Lbls}[\mathbb{L}\mapsto j])
.

•

If
\alpha=p{?}q(\mskip-4.0mu(j)\mskip-4.0mu)
, then
\mathtt{RTs}(q)\mathrel{\circeq}(q\lozenge r){!}p^{\mathbb{L}}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\lBrace i\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}.\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{i}\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rBrace_{i\in I}
with
j\in I
.
If
\not\exists\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\in\dom(\mathtt{Lbls}).~\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\mathbin{\Cap}\mathbb{L}
, then
\Omega^{\prime}=\LO(p,\mathtt{RTs}[q\mapsto\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{j}],\mathtt{Lbls}[\mathbb{L}\mapsto j])
.
If
\exists(\mathbb{L}^{\prime},j)\in\mathtt{Lbls}.~\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\mathbin{\Cap}\mathbb{L}
, then
\Omega^{\prime}=\LO(p,\mathtt{RTs}[q\mapsto\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R_{j}],\mathtt{Lbls})
.

•

If
\alpha=\mathsf{end}
, then
\forall(q,\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R)\in\mathtt{RTs}.~\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}R\mathrel{\circeq}\mathsf{end}
, and
\Omega^{\prime}=\LO(p,\emptyset,\emptyset)
.

•

If
\alpha=\tau
, then
\Omega^{\prime}=\Omega=\LO(p,\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})
.

Proof

Keeping in mind
lemma

21
,
\Omega
is generated with each possible
\alpha
appearing exactly once under specific conditions and with unique continuation.

∎

Theorem 8.3
(Transparency)

Suppose given

•

a well-typed global type
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}
,

•

a participant
p\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})
, and

•

a blackbox
P_{0}
.

Let

•

\mathtt{RTs}_{0}:=\{(q,\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathbin{\rangle}(p,q)^{\{\varepsilon\}})\mid q\in\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})\setminus\{p\}\}
, and

•

\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0}:=\gtToMon(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0},p,\prt(\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0})\setminus\{p\})
.

Suppose
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\triangleright\mathtt{RTs}_{0}\mathbin{@}p
minimally (
definition

14
).

Let
\Omega_{0}:=\LO(p,\mathtt{RTs}_{0},\emptyset)
.
Then
{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}\mathrel{\approx_{\Omega_{0}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}
.

Proof

By
Definition 8
(Satisfaction)
, there exists a minimal satisfaction
\mathcal{R}
at
p
such that
({\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]},\mathtt{RTs}_{0},\emptyset)\in\mathcal{R}
.
Let

\mathcal{B}:=\{\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}({\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]},\Omega,{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{n},\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle})\\
{}\mid\forall P,M,\vec{m},\vec{n}.~\exists\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls}.~\big(\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}({\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]},\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})\in\mathcal{R}\\
{}\wedge G_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}p\mathrel{\bowtie}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathrel{\bowtie}\mathtt{RTs}\mathrel{\bowtie}\vec{n}\\
{}\wedge\Omega=\LO(p,\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})\big)\}.\end{array}\end{array}

Clearly,
({\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]},\Omega_{0},{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P_{0}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle})\in\mathcal{B}
, with
P=P_{0},\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M=\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0},\vec{m}=\vec{n}=\varepsilon,\mathtt{RTs}=\mathtt{RTs}_{0},\mathtt{Lbls}=\emptyset
, and clearly
G_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}p\mathrel{\bowtie}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}\mathtt{RTs}_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}\varepsilon
.

It remains to show that
\mathcal{B}
is a weak bisimulation.
Take any
(\mathcal{P},\Omega,\mathcal{Q})\in\mathcal{B}
: there are
P,\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M,\vec{n},\vec{m}
such that
\mathcal{P}={\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}[}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}:}\vec{n}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}]}
,
\mathcal{Q}={\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{n},\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}
, and there are
\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls}
such that
(\mathcal{P},\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})\in\mathcal{R}
,
\color[rgb]{0.74,0.29,0}G_{0}\mathrel{\bowtie}p\mathrel{\bowtie}\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M\mathrel{\bowtie}\mathtt{RTs}\mathrel{\bowtie}\vec{n}
, and
\Omega=\LO(p,\mathtt{RTs},\mathtt{Lbls})
.
We show that the two conditions of
definition

12
hold.

1.

Take any
\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\alpha,\Omega_{1}
such that
\mathcal{P}\mathrel{{}_{\Omega}{\mathrel{\hbox to12.89pt{\vbox to12.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.4438pt\lower-7.75134pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

.
The analysis depends on the rule from
definition

used to derive the transition (Transition [

], [

], or [

]).
We never need to read extra messages, so in each case we show the thesis for
\vec{b}:=\varepsilon
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}:=\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
.

Transition [

The analysis depends on the derivation of the transition.
Some rules are impossible: there is no parallel composition in
\mathcal{P}
, no rules to derive transitions for buffered blackboxes are possible, and, by
lemma

14
, no transitions resulting in an error signal are possible.

Some rules require to first unfold recursion in
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M
, derived by a number of consecutive applications of Transition [
mon-rec
].
We apply induction on this number.
The inductive case is trivial by the IH.

In the base case, where there are no applications of Transition [
mon-rec
], we consider each possible rule (Transition [
mon-out
], [
mon-in

mon-in-dep
], [
mon-tau
], [

•

Transition [
dep
].
Then
\alpha=\tau
,
\mathcal{Q}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\alpha^{\prime}$}}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
with
\alpha^{\prime}=s{!}r(\ell)
for some
s,r,\ell
.
There are no rules to derive this transition, so this case does not apply.

•

Transition [
no-dep
].
Then
\alpha\neq s{!}r(\ell)
for any
s,r,\ell
,
\mathcal{Q}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\alpha$}}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
, and
\Omega_{1}=\Omega(\alpha)
.
The analysis depends on the derivation of the transition.
Some rules are impossible: there is no parallel composition in
\mathcal{Q}
, and no rules to derive transitions for monitored blackboxes are possible.

As in case 1 above, we may first need to unfold recursion in
\color[rgb]{0.16,0.23,0.42}M
, which we do inductively.
We consider each possible rule (Transition [
buf-out
], [
buf-in
], [
buf-in-dep
], [
buf-tau
], and [
buf-end
]).

–

Transition [
buf-out
].
Then
\alpha=p{!}q(j\langle T_{j}\rangle)
,
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\langle}p\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}P^{\prime}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}:}\vec{n},\vec{m}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\rangle}
, and
P\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\alpha$}}P^{\prime}
.
Then, also by Transition [
buf-out
],
